**⚠ ANYTHING UNVERIFIED IS MARKED `UNKNOWN`.** An invented entry is worse than a gap: a gap gets asked about, an invention gets built.

## ⚠⚠ HOW THIS FILE IS ORDERED (Justin's ruling 2026-08-28)

**THE ORDER IS THE PRIORITY: 🐛 BUG → ▪ MINOR → ▰ MAJOR.** Within the feature sections his stated queue order stands: **admin view rebuild part 2 → first-time password page → Zoom → role hierarchy.**

| tag | means |
|---|---|
| ✅ **CROSS-ACCOUNT INGESTION — CLEANED UP AND THE CAUSE FIXED (2026-08-31, `2a3a0e2`)** | **The 41 are deleted with everything derived** (20 analyses, 97 highlights, 18 prospects, 3 KB rows, 10 stale cache rows). Deleted against the **isolated verified id set, never re-derived**; the **21 pending went first**; every delta matched the blast radius measured beforehand and **everyone else's 1,764 calls were untouched**. Nathan 168 -> **127, exactly his own**; Dre 54 -> 54. Cross-ownership re-checked: **the pair is gone.** | ⚠⚠ **THE RANKING WAS THE DEFECT, NOT THE USER** — suggestions were ordered by who had recorded most of the last ten WORKSPACE meetings, a fact about recent activity with no relationship to who is connecting (Dre 7 of 10 offered first, Nathan's own address third). **Root fix is upstream: auto-resolution read `/team_members` only, and his address was in `recorded_by` on `/meetings` all along** — `identityCandidates()` merges both. ⚠ **The matching rule did NOT relax: exact equality only, ambiguity still refuses; the CANDIDATE LIST widens, the COMPARISON never does.** Picker now ranks by affinity to the signed-in address, an exact match is pinned first and pre-filled as **the answer**, the misleading activity count is gone, and the screen says what picking wrong does. **Live-verified: Nathan and Gabriel now resolve; `josh@scoutsystems.io` correctly still does not** (his Fathom identity genuinely differs from his login), which is why the ranking is defence in depth. | ⚠⚠ **STILL OPEN — THE STRUCTURAL HOLE, DESIGNED NOT BUILT: a wrong-identity ingestion has no undo, and the blocker is that it is UNDETECTABLE** — `fathom_calls` records nothing about which identity fetched a row, so nothing could have surfaced this. **DETECTION BEFORE DELETION: (1) stamp `recorded_by` at ingestion (additive, new rows only, no backfill); (2) then *calls whose recorder is not the owner's identity* is a one-query invariant that must read zero; (3) only then a repair path behind an explicit confirmed admin action.** ⚠ **Deleting calls on disconnect is explicitly NOT the answer** — the destructive behaviour the preserve-history ruling prevents. **Retraction-on-identity-change cannot be built until the stamp exists.** ⚠ **§5 audited: no third case** — 7 of 9 exact, josh/joshua known-correct, one null. |
| ✅ **Model spend is now attributed — SHIPPED 2026-08-31 (`15e0d4d`, migration 059)** | Anthropic returns token counts on every response and Scout **discarded all of it**. `lib/model-usage.js` logs `(user_id, fathom_call_id, lane, model, input, output, ok)` per response. **Additive, new calls only, no backfill** — past counts were never captured and a guessed number would make a cost report look complete. | ⚠⚠ **THE BRIEF SAID "ONE SEAM" AND THERE WERE ELEVEN** — every module had its own identical `getAnthropic()`, **verified identical before consolidating.** The lane is **bound at import**, so 21 call sites stay one token and cannot drift. ⚠ **It can never break the call it measures** (failures swallowed, write not awaited) and **a FAILED call is still recorded with `ok=false`** — a lane that errors repeatedly is a spend question too. ⚠ `routes/proxy.js` **deliberately exempt** (dormant teleprompter). | ⚠⚠ **CARRIED SO IT IS NOT RE-DERIVED: the grader and extractor EACH send the full transcript — ~95% of an analysis; coaching sends moments only, ~5%. Merging the two was measured and REFUSED ON QUALITY.** The unexplored lever is whether every lane needs the largest model — **every lane runs Sonnet and none has ever been evaluated on a smaller one.** The logging now makes that testable. |
| ✅ **Time to price — SHIPPED 2026-08-31 (`ab75f71`). Justin's premise was correct and is now the implementation.** | The graph shows one line because `findPriceMoment` is **skipped entirely unless `price_pif > 0`**, and **only 2 of 13 users have one — both Josh Pinner's accounts.** Every other rep: **0 moments across 750+ graded calls.** | ⚠⚠ **IT DOES NOT NEED A STORED PRICE, AND THE DISCRIMINATOR IS ALREADY SHIPPED.** `PRICE_FRAME_RE` (total-framing language) is what separates a price from a decoy — the module's own header measured **85% vs 0-1%**. A priceless detector using that regex **agrees with the stored lookup on 59 of 60 ground-truth calls (98%, median delta 0s)** and takes five more reps from **0% to 23-56%**. ⚠⚠ **And the 13% "false positive" rate INVERTS on reading: five of the first eight are the real $9,800 stated as a total** — moments the stored version MISSES, exactly Justin's multiple-packages point. **A disagreement rate is not an error rate until you read the disagreements.** ⚠ **UNESTABLISHED, not guessed: why 48% are null today** — not a line-level detection failure (3 of 4 sampled ARE found in isolation) and not a backfill gap (786 of 788 analysed after the feature shipped). **RECOMMENDATION: replace the stored-price lookup with the framing detector.** |
| ✅ **Graph axis showed one date in seven — FIXED 2026-08-31 (`4813649`)** | Justin reported *"no dates along the bottom"* three times. **Neither obvious diagnosis was right** — not the Monday rule misfiring (measured: it labels exactly the real Mondays) and not the axis rendering nothing. | ⚠⚠ **THE RULE WAS CORRECT AND APPLIED TOO WIDELY.** Monday-only thinning exists because Chart.js drops labels SILENTLY when they collide (~30 labels at ~36px against ~48px needed) — but it ran on EVERY daily range, and **the team default is SEVEN DAYS, which contains exactly ONE Monday.** One label at 157px of room each reads as no dates. ⚠ The threshold is **derived from the reasoning the comment already gave** and keys on **width per label, not day count**, so a narrow chart still thins at 14 days. **7 days now shows 7 (was 1), 30 still thins to 5.** ⚠ **All three graphs share one builder**, so the third — which nobody reported — was fixed with them. |
| ✅ **Fathom identity — no who-am-I endpoint (closed), full paging, and `recorded_by` stamped (2026-08-31, `94081f0`)** | ⚠⚠ **DOES FATHOM EXPOSE THE AUTHENTICATED USER? NO — CLOSED BY LIVE PROBE, DO NOT RE-OPEN.** Sixteen identity paths 404, `/users` 403, and the three endpoints that answer (`/teams`, `/team_members`, `/meeting_types`) carry **no self-identifying field**. **The access token is OPAQUE — one segment, not a JWT** — so no embedded subject, and the grant is `scope=public_api`, not OIDC. **The picker work is the answer, not a workaround.** | ⚠⚠⚠ **AND THE PROBE FOUND THE REAL ROOT CAUSE, CORRECTING MY OWN DIAGNOSIS: `/team_members` IS PAGINATED — 32 members across 4 pages, and Nathan is #11, on PAGE TWO.** He was never absent; the resolver read page one and answered `no_match` from an incomplete input. **A pagination limit presenting as an absence** — third instance, and the tell was free (`next_cursor` + `limit` in the body). `fetchAllPages` follows the cursor, **bounded at 20 pages**, returning what it has on a failed page so an incomplete list degrades to the picker rather than breaking a connect. **Live-verified: Nathan, Gabriel AND Nick now resolve — 3 of 4, up from 2; Nick is the one the source-merge could not fix.** `josh@scoutsystems.io` **correctly** still does not (his Fathom identity genuinely differs from his login). | ⚠⚠ **STEP 1 OF §3 DONE — migration 058 stamps `recorded_by` at ingestion, NEW ROWS ONLY, no backfill** (the value is unrecoverable historically and **a guessed stamp would report a clean audit over rows it never checked**). It stores **Fathom's own value, not our filter argument** — a disagreement is the thing worth seeing. **`scripts/audit-call-ownership.js` makes the invariant a one-query check** (mismatched must be 0; currently 0 stamped / 1,945 unstamped / 0 mismatched) and **keeps three states apart — matching · mismatched · never stamped** — because folding NULL either way is the absent-vs-excluded collapse. **READ-ONLY, pinned by a test forbidding delete/update/upsert.** ⚠ **Repair path NOT built — detection first, and it will be explicit and confirmed. Deleting calls on disconnect stays REJECTED, recorded in the migration itself.** |
| `🐛 BUG` | something that does not work and needs to |
| `▪ MINOR` | a button, a graph, a small capability |
| `▰ MAJOR` | Marketing Insights, Scout AI, whole pages |

**⚠⚠ THIS SUPERSEDES THE PREVIOUS NOTE THAT SAID "TYPE IS A SECOND AXIS — THE FILE IS NOT RE-SECTIONED BY IT, DELIBERATELY."** That note argued sectioning by type would force rows into two places. Justin ruled the other way on 2026-08-28 because the state-based sections had become unreadable. **The old note is not kept as an alternative — it is wrong now, and a file carrying both would be a second live answer.**

**Counts after the 2026-08-28 reorder — `🔴 NEEDS JUSTIN` 18 · `🐛 BUGS` 12 · `▪ MINOR` 52 · `▰ MAJOR` 9 · `⏳ TRIGGERED` 6 · `🗄 CLOSED` 50 · `✅ LIVE` 63.** **All 205 original rows carried over, none duplicated — verified mechanically, not by eye.**

**⚠ THE RULE THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE ORDER: A ROW APPEARS EXACTLY ONCE.** If a row needs Justin, it is in **NEEDS JUSTIN** and nowhere else. Anything ruled, shipped or superseded is **out of the forward-looking sections** and lives in HISTORY at the bottom.

### 💷 WHAT IT COSTS TO RUN — MEASURED 2026-09-01, NOT ESTIMATED

**38 real analysed calls through `model_usage`. The first real numbers Justin has had.**

```
one analysed call     17.6c measured  ->  ~19.6c corrected
   grader     9.2c   every call     the two large lanes are 93% OF THE MONEY,
   extractor  7.1c   every call     because each receives the FULL transcript
   coaching   1.3c   71% of calls   (merging them was measured and REFUSED on quality)

one rep / month, at real August volumes
   lightest  54 calls  $10.60    5% of a $199 seat
   typical  126 calls  $24.74   12%
   heaviest 269 calls  $52.82   27%
   the nine-rep team: 1,203 calls = $236/mo against $1,791 of seats = 13%
```

- **⚠ THE MEASURED FIGURE UNDERSTATES IT AND THE CORRECTION IS THE HONEST PART.** The 38 sampled
  calls have a **43-minute median against the corpus's 48**, and cost tracks length almost
  exactly — so the headline is ~20c, not 17.6c. **A sample that is unrepresentative in a
  measurable way must be corrected for, not quoted raw.**
- **$199 IS COMFORTABLE, NOT TIGHT.** A rep would need **~1,000 calls a month** to consume a
  seat. ⚠ **This is the AI cost ONLY** — hosting, database and embeddings are extra, small and
  fixed rather than per-rep, and unmeasured.
- **⚠⚠ THE ONE LEVER, REPORTED AND DELIBERATELY NOT TESTED: whether the two large lanes need the
  largest model.** The smaller model's published price is a third, so a call would go ~20c → ~7c
  and a typical rep $25 → ~$9. **The risk is real and specific:** the grader produces every
  number Scout shows and already moves ±14 points per section run-to-run on one model, so a
  cheaper one cannot be validated by watching scores; and the extractor's verbatim-quote contract
  went 67% → 93% only after careful work, which a weaker model may not hold. **Justin rules.**

| block | state |
|---|---|
| **① type scale** | ✅ **LIVE `131a12b`.** 7 sizes, 3 weights, 3 radii. On Performance: **23 size/weight pairs → 11**, weights `400,450,500,600,700 → 400,500`. **The 115 semibolds are gone from that page** |
| **② borders → space + THE GROUND** | ⚠ **BUILT, COMMITTED (`146eb47`, `f396fa5`), NOT PUSHED — awaiting Justin's look.** The blocker is RESOLVED: he ruled option 2 and the ground is in. **Exposed text 95 of 105 → 0 of 102; contrast measured per line against the ground each line sits on — worst 11.09:1, none below AA.** ⚠⚠ **SAFETY IS LAYOUT, NOT FAINTNESS** — recorded at the code as a standing rule re-applied, so nobody later dims the raster instead. A SURFACE not a card (no border/radius/nesting, verified on the rendered page), in the page's OWN `--bg`, at the content column's width so the artwork keeps the gutters (360px at 1920 → 0 at ≤1200, the column filling the screen). ⚠ **THE GROUND IS THE PATTERN THE REST OF THE PASS DEPENDS ON** — every page that loses its cards inherits the same exposure. ⚠ **PROPOSED NOT TAKEN: a bigger dial.** The ground did not change the gauge answer — the tightness is between the number and the dial's inner edge, so a backdrop could never move it |
| **③ the other four pages · ④ sidebar + logo** | not started |

**RULINGS APPLIED:** page title takes the 20px step (it must outrank a section heading) · the 10px caret and 9px dial ticks are a **NAMED EXEMPTION**, recorded in the code and a test so a sweep finds a decision not a stray · the gauges take the display step.

**⚠ THE DISPLAY SIZE WAS COMPARED BEFORE IT WAS CHOSEN — 48/34/28/20 side by side.** 34 sits most comfortably in the dial; **34 is not on the scale and an eighth size is what "nothing between them, ever" forbids**, so 48 ships with the caveat stated at the code: the number's width approaches the dial's inner edge and `46min` is the tightest case. **If it reads cramped the fix is a BIGGER DIAL, never an eighth size.**

**⚠⚠ TWO HEADINGS HAD NO CSS RULE AT ALL** and rendered at the browser's default (21px bold — the loudest text on the page, decided by nobody). Found by asking the rendered page which rule matched and getting back **none**, twice. A `.section h2` floor makes an unstyled heading impossible.

**⚠⚠ THE BLOCKER ON ②, AND IT IS JUSTIN'S:** removing the cards removed the only opaque ground between the text and the background raster — **95 of 105 text elements on Performance are now exposed, where it was ZERO** — and the raster runs at **full brightness** on the strength of a sweep that found 13 of 15 views had no exposed text. **Visible, not theoretical:** the graph headings sit on a bright band of the mesh. **Two fixes, both design calls: dim/drop the raster on this view, or give the page a solid ground so "no cards" ≠ "no surface".** Recommendation is the second; not taken.

⚠ **The contrast probe was unreliable and is not quoted** — it read the brightest pixel anywhere in the source image and returned a figure contradicting the screenshot. **The count is solid; the ratio is not.**

### 📐 THE DESIGN PASS — SIZED, NOT STARTED (2026-08-31)

**Three to five blocks, and it cannot share a session.**

```
where style lives     13 files — and 9 of them load the shared stylesheet
dashboard.html alone  374 font-size · 183 font-weight · 85 border-radius
type tokens           NONE. every one of those 374 sizes is a literal
radius tokens         3 exist — and 94 declarations IGNORE them vs 76 that use them,
                      at values the tokens do not offer (3,4,5,6,10,14px)
weights               115 share ONE value; 7 distinct in total
sizes                 12 distinct, including half-pixels (12.5 / 10.5 / 13.5)
```

**⚠ TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE AUDIT:** the app and login do **NOT** run different typefaces — they were unified when Saira shipped; what diverges is **`admin.html` on monospace**. And there are **THREE** blacks, not two: dashboard `#0a0a0a`, `css/style.css` `#080b0d`, login `#000`.

**⚠⚠ SWEEP OR REBUILD — IT IS BOTH, WHICH IS THE REASON FOR THE BLOCK COUNT.** **Radii are a sweep** (the tokens exist; map ~94 literals onto 3, each a judgement). **Type is a REBUILD** — there is nothing to sweep *onto*, so a scale must be created first and 374 declarations each assigned a step; that is the bulk. **Weights are a decision then a sweep** (115 on one value is too FEW distinctions, not too many). **Blacks and the admin typeface are small.** **Separation-by-border-vs-space is a LAYOUT change, not a token change**, and the likeliest to look wrong in ways only a screenshot shows.

**⚠ Every decision in it is visual, so it needs pages looked at rather than declarations counted.**

**One team page became five behind a dropdown: Daily Digest (default landing) · Performance · Coaching · Objections · People.** Shipped `24c6e93`, deploy confirmed by commit hash. **Suite 1916, zero failures.**

|---|---|

| **⚠⚠ Customize View MIGRATES** | the panel keys are unchanged and each gained a page — **that IS the migration.** Proven on the real page: a set stored before the split survives and both hidden panels stay hidden **on their new pages**. Namespacing would have reset every manager's board |

| **Every page says which window it governs** | resolved from `TEAM_PAGES`, so the control and the caption cannot drift |

1. **the chart mount landed in the Coaching renderer**, which has no graphs — caught by the test that drives the render path, not the one that drives the function
2. **`viewToHashPath` had no cases for the new pages**, so the URL stopped naming the page and a refresh lost it. **The hash has a read side and a write side and only one fails loudly**
3. **People's picker silently moved Performance's window** while its own caption said *"These dates apply to People"* — a write through a read-shaped fallback

**⚠⚠ TWO OF MY OWN GUARDS WERE VACUOUS AND WERE REPLACED AFTER BEING PROVEN SO:** *"both routers carry every page"* counted the page name **anywhere in the file** — and it also occurs in the URL builder, so deleting a whole router line still left two; *"every page is reachable by hash"* checked only the **read** side, so deleting both write cases left it green. **Eight defect classes now fail on demand.** A third gap the same sweep exposed: one router reached the digest through a **catch-all `else`**, which made the multiple-dispatch trap invisible there.

**⚠ ONE OBSERVATION FOR JUSTIN, REPORTED NOT RESOLVED:** the **gauges (fixed 7 days) and the graphs (picker-driven) both land on Performance**, so the two-windows-on-one-page contradiction survives the split. The dials' caption states it explicitly. **Moving the dials to Daily Digest would dissolve it — a product call.**

### ✅ RULED 2026-08-28 — MOVING A REP DOES **NOT** MOVE THEIR HISTORY

**⚠ This was carried as "SECOND QUESTION FOR JUSTIN" and it is ANSWERED. The heading is corrected rather than left reading as open** — a decided question sitting under a question heading is exactly what gets re-asked. The measurement below is unchanged and is why the ruling costs work.

**`fathom_calls` carries only `user_id`, and team scope resolves `managed_by` at query time.** So reassigning a rep **retroactively moves every past quarter's calls, cash, close rate and objections** from one company's totals to the other. Nothing records which team a call belonged to when it happened.

**Reported and NOT changed** — the fix is either freezing history (snapshot the team on each call) or accepting the retroactive move, and that is a **reporting decision**. ⚠ It also compounds with the naming caveat: a client with two sales managers is already two companies, so "move a rep between managers" and "move a rep between companies" are the same action today.

**⚠⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-08-28 — JUSTIN HAS RULED, AND THE ANSWER IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT THIS FILE PREVIOUSLY RECORDED.** **MOVING A REP DOES NOT MOVE THEIR HISTORY: call data STAYS WITH THE COMPANY WHERE IT WAS EARNED.** The 2026-08-25 entry that accepted *copying* history to the new team — and accepted the resulting double-count — **is dead. Do not implement it, and do not leave both readings on file.**

**The consequence is unchanged and is the whole cost:** `fathom_calls` carries only `user_id` and team scope resolves `managed_by` **live**, so honouring the ruling needs **a team stamp written at call creation** plus **a sweep of every team-scoped query** to key on the stamp rather than on today's membership; backfill the stamp from current mapping as a best guess. ⚠ It is the same mechanism the **offer axis** needs — whoever builds either should build both.

### ⚠⚠ WHAT REMAINS — AND THE RULING THAT UNBLOCKS IT

Login sizes it `min(96vw, 1038px)`; **1038px is the asset's native width**, above which the browser upscales. ⚠ **The reveal control is a real a11y surface**, not a styling job: it needs an accessible name that changes with state, and it must not live only in a `title` attribute. |

**⚠⚠ THE CAVEAT ANSWERED — THIS IS A DISPLAY CHANGE, NOT A BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, *FOR THE RULE THAT EXISTS TODAY.*** Measured 2026-08-24, the question the architect flagged:

```
POST /auth/set-password      password.length < 10  -> 400   ENFORCED SERVER-SIDE
                             password.length > 200 -> 400   ENFORCED SERVER-SIDE
POST /auth/change-password   same two bounds                ENFORCED SERVER-SIDE
set-password.html            placeholder "At least 10 characters" + a client check
dashboard.html (#account)    the same placeholder + the same client check
```

So **"at least 10 characters" is already the rule and is already enforced** — writing it under the field only stops it being a placeholder the user loses the moment they type. **Nothing changes behaviourally.**

**⚠ BUT ANY RULE BEYOND THOSE TWO IS NEW ENFORCEMENT.** An uppercase / number / symbol requirement is **not** enforced anywhere today, server or client. Adding it to the displayed list means implementing it in **both** password endpoints — and it would **lock out no existing user** (the rules apply on set, not on login), but it **is** a behaviour change and must be ruled on as one. **Display only what is enforced, or enforce what you display; never show a rule the server does not apply.**

### 3 · ZOOM — A FIX, NOT A REBUILD (diagnosed 2026-08-26)

**⚠ The original 2026-08-22 headline ("Zoom produces no usable data") was DISPROVEN. It is kept as history at the bottom of this file and must never sit above this section.**


| feature | Zoom | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **Grading** | **WORKS** | same meeting, both providers: 67/63 · 74/74 · 73/73 · 70/71 · 67/67 · 55/56 |
| **EOD figures** | **WORKS** | outcome + `eod_summary` on 48/48 |
| **Team analytics** | **WORKS** | same tables |
| **Prospect name** | **DEGRADED** | **81%** (34/42) vs Fathom 99% — 20 diarized, 20 grader, **0 from title** |
| **Highlights** | **DEGRADED** | fine under an hour; **7 of 8 calls over 60 min have ZERO** (Fathom: 0 of 176) |
| **Objections** | **DEGRADED** | 0.43/call vs 0.93 — downstream of highlights |
| **Clips** | **DEGRADED** | `recording_url` on 53/53, but no timestamp deep-link |
| **Speaker split** | **DEGRADED** | 6 of 39 calls collapse into one turn |

**⚠ The transcript is NOT empty and IS parsed** — 775 chars/min vs Fathom's 841 (92%). The difference is granularity (177 chars/turn vs 53), and **that does NOT hurt quote accuracy: Zoom verifies at 97%, Fathom 92%.**

**⚠⚠ THE LONG-CALL HIGHLIGHT FAULT DOES NOT REPRODUCE** — re-running the extractor on the two worst calls returned 8 moments each. Ruled out by measurement: empty/unparsed transcript, input size, timestamps past the hour, newlines, output truncation, rate-limit burst. **The mechanism is undeterminable because an extractor failure records NO reason — that gap is the blocker, and it is already filed below.** Re-analysing those 8 calls would recover their highlights; **not done, spend not approved.**

### ORDERED — hardest first. ⚠ Items 1-3 and 5 are ENGINEERING; item 4 depends on how the closer runs meetings.
1. **Clip extraction** — **neither the download nor the cutting step exists.** Zoom has no jump-to-a-moment link, so this is the only route to parity. ⚠ **The published privacy policy already describes it as if it were built.** ⚠⚠ **THE STORAGE DECISION IS NOW FILED IN NEEDS JUSTIN (2026-08-29)** — a new bucket, video mime types, a size cap and a retention rule, for **886 moments across 144 Zoom calls**. Do not start the pipeline before that ruling.
2. ~~**Record why an extraction failed**~~ — ✅ **SHIPPED, THIS ROW WAS STALE (verified 2026-08-29).** `call_analyses.highlight_error` is written on every analysis that reaches the step (`lib/analysis-worker.js`, three write sites incl. `persist_failed:`) and is READ by `lib/health-snapshot.js`. **Live in prod: 6 of 1,868 rows carry a reason.** ⚠ So the long-call fault can now be diagnosed from the row rather than guessed at — which is what item 1's investigation was waiting on.
3. ~~**Stop a one-speaker call collapsing into one turn**~~ — ⚠⚠ **INVESTIGATED 2026-08-29: THIS IS NOT AN ENGINEERING FIX, AND THE SCALE IS SMALLER THAN THE ROW IMPLIED.** **17 of 174 real Zoom calls collapse (9.8%), but only FOUR carry substantial content** — the other 13 are trivial or test meetings (10 under 500 characters, several 0-duration). Fathom control: 1 of 1,287. ⚠ **THE ADAPTER IS NOT AT FAULT, proven on the same accounts:** Godwin averages **363 turns** across 126 calls (max 1,285) and Josh 221 across 42 — so the parser demonstrably separates speakers for those very users. The collapse is per-CALL. ⚠ **THE CAUSE IS ONE AUDIO CHANNEL, read from the real transcripts rather than the VTT spec:** every collapsed call is a two-sided conversation under the host's single label, and two of them narrate the workaround out loud — *"our usual Zoom issue… I'm gonna call you, Aaron"* — the host PHONES the other party, so both voices arrive on the host's own channel and Zoom attributes all of it to the host. **No adapter change can split one channel.** ⚠ **The distribution corrects an easy misreading: sorting by transcript size puts Godwin's calls on top, but he has the LOWEST rate (3%); Josh is 17% and the reviewer's 6 test meetings are 100%.** ⚠⚠ **WHAT IS ACTUALLY WORTH FIXING IS THE CONSEQUENCE, NOT THE CAUSE: those 4 calls are still GRADED AND SCORED (71, 47, 32) with `speaker_closer_name` NULL and 5-8 highlights extracted** — a confident score built on a transcript where the model was told one person said everything, including the prospect's objections. **Filed, not built: whether a single-speaker transcript should be graded at all is a ruling.**
4. ~~**The Personal Meeting Room** *(not engineering)*~~ — ⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-29 BY THE CALL-TITLE ROW AT THE BOTTOM OF MINOR. DO NOT READ THIS AS DECIDED AGAINST.** The original reasoning — *45 of 53 calls share one title, so booking through a calendar link fixes it at source* — **treated the TITLE as the only possible source of a prospect's name.** ⚠ **The new route ignores the title entirely and reads the name from the TRANSCRIPT**, which Scout already does: its own coaching on these very calls says *"When Scott said 'I need to talk it over with my girlfriend'"* while the title still reads "Godwin Ona's Personal Meeting Room". **So it does NOT depend on the closer changing how he runs meetings**, which is what made the original filing a non-engineering item.
5. **Turn granularity** — measured NOT to hurt quality. **Leave it alone.**

**Every one of Godwin's calls reads "Godwin Ona's Personal Meeting Room", while Scout's own coaching on the same call says *"When Scott said 'I need to talk it over with my girlfriend'"*.** ⚠⚠ **It already knows who the call was with and never uses that to title anything.**

| item | state | what it needs |
|---|---|---|
| **Derive the title from the transcript when the meeting title carries no name** | ⚠ **FILED, NOT BUILT** | ⚠ **The machinery already exists and is proven** — `call_analyses.prospect_name` is populated by the grader and by `lib/prospect-name.js`, whose precedence is **grader → diarized → cleaned title → NULL**. What is missing is any surface USING it as a title when the title itself is a meeting-room label. ⚠⚠ **THE GOVERNING RULE ALREADY ON FILE APPLIES UNCHANGED: a WRONG name is worse than NO name.** A meeting-room label, a device name or a dial-in id must resolve to the existing fallback, never to a plausible guess — that rule is why `prospect_name` is trustworthy at all. |
| **Let anyone rename a call from the Calls page** | ⚠ **FILED, NOT BUILT** | A human override on the title, which needs a stored column and an edit control. ⚠ **It must follow the read-before-write pattern the outcome and exclusion paths already use**: a re-analysis must never overwrite a name a person typed, and the field that records WHO set it is what makes that override durable. |

**⚠⚠ THIS SUPERSEDES THE "Personal Meeting Room — not engineering" ITEM IN THE ZOOM SECTION**, which was reasoned from the title being the only available source. That row is struck in place so nobody reads it and concludes this was decided against.

### N · TWO RULINGS ON THE KNOWLEDGE-BASE ARC (Justin, 2026-08-29 — FILED, NOT BUILT)

**Both are steps in the same plan whose step 2 shipped as the manager Add-to-KB control.**

| item | state | what it needs, and the reasoning worth keeping |
|---|---|---|
| **Reps can SUGGEST a moment for their manager to approve** | ⚠ **RULED, NOT BUILT** | A rep marks a moment; it queues for the manager rather than entering the KB. ⚠⚠ **EXPLICITLY NOT A REVERSAL OF THE 2026-08-18 REMOVAL, and the distinction is the whole reason it is allowed back:** that removal deleted a rep button **duplicating auto-harvest** — measured 313 automatic rows against **0** manual ones in two weeks. **Suggesting UPWARD has no automatic equivalent**, so it is a new capability rather than a restored duplicate. ⚠ It also re-opens the path the removal knowingly closed: *there is currently NO route from a rep's call moment into the TEAM KB*, because auto-harvest writes `scope:'personal'` and `lib/kb-scope.js` means a manager cannot SEE those rows to promote them. |
| **Reps get their own "Add to Personal Knowledge Base" button** | ⚠ **RULED, NOT BUILT** | Same reasoning: **marking for one's OWN KB has no automatic equivalent either.** Auto-harvest gates on `outcome='closed'` ALONE (ruling 4) and caps ~2 per section per call — so a moment on a call that did not close, or a third moment in one section, is unreachable by any automatic path. ⚠ The write itself is nearly free: `lib/kb-entry.js` `buildMomentRow` + `insertMoment` already exist and the partial unique index makes a repeat add **idempotent by construction**. |
| **Surfacing coachable moments: MANAGER DIGEST FIRST, THEN A MOMENTS FEED** | ⚠ **ORDER RULED, NEITHER BUILT** | ⚠⚠ **The order is the ruling — do not build the feed first.** The digest already exists, already runs once per manager per day on the sync cron, and already resolves evidence from real rows with rep attribution; adding coachable moments to it is an addition to a working surface. **A feed is a new surface with its own routing, filtering and empty states**, and this project has repeatedly found that improving the surface a user already reads beats adding one beside it — the same lesson as the three Call Review panels that were removed for being extra sections rather than better ones. |

### N · "DOESN'T WANT IT AT ALL" HAS NO ROUTE — A FOURTH CASE THE BOUNDARY MISSES (measured 2026-08-29, **REPORT ONLY, NOT BUILT**)

**The three-way boundary covers affordability, external blockers and hesitancy. A prospect who CAN pay and simply does not want the offer satisfies the `fear` rule exactly — and `fear` is the coachable one.**

| item | state | the evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **A genuine no-need is routed to `fear` and counted against the rep** | ✅ **FIXED 2026-08-30 as v35 (`004c033`) — the boundary now asks whether they WANT it before whether they CAN buy** | The extractor's test is **affordability-only** (*"can the prospect buy right now if they decide to? If they genuinely CANNOT AFFORD IT…"*); `disqualify_signal` is defined as *"no budget, no decision authority, wrong stage"* — **no BUDGET, never no NEED**; and `fear` explicitly claims the remainder (*"wanting more proof… wanting to think about it… are all fear when the prospect can afford it"*). ⚠⚠ **Measured on 30 prospect-spoken no-need moments it scatters across SIX types with no rule routing it:** `risk_signal 10 · buying_signal 7 · objection/fear 6 · disqualify_signal 4 · objection/logistical 2 · missed_opportunity 1` — **7 of 30 land somewhere that counts against the rep, 4 land correctly.** ⚠⚠ **The proof is a pair saying the same thing and routing opposite ways:** *"I have an MBA. Do I really need this? I don't need this."* → **`objection/fear`**; *"I have a company that's making $3 million a year, so I don't need this headache"* → correctly **`disqualify_signal`**. ⚠ **Honest limit: keyword sample, it over-matches** (*"I don't need to finance it"* is a real buying signal it caught) — **the counts are illustrative, not a rate. The structural finding follows from the three definitions and does not depend on them.** ⚠ **Coaching a rep for not closing someone who should never have bought is actively wrong**, and it silently depresses the handle rate on calls where the rep did nothing wrong. | **✅ THE FIX:** TEST 1 — *do they want it at all?* A **stated reason the offer does not apply** → `disqualify_signal`, never `objection`. TEST 2 runs **only if they want it** — v27's ability question, three-way boundary untouched. `disqualify_signal` gains **no need**; without that door the want question had nowhere to route to. ⚠⚠ **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS A STATED REASON, NOT THE WORDS "I don't need it"** — both kinds of prospect say them. *"I have a company making $3 million a year, so I don't need this headache"* carries a reason → DQ; a bare *"do I really need this?"* from an engaged prospect is **a stall wearing a no-need costume** → stays `fear`. **With no reason to point at, the prompt DEFAULTS TO THE OBJECTION** — `fear` is the only coachable category, so failing toward it is the safety property, and a guard pins it. ⚠⚠ **BOTH CHECKS RUN BEFORE PUSHING, AND THE CONTROL ARM IS WHAT MADE THEM MEAN ANYTHING.** ① On an **adversarial** sample (10 calls selected BECAUSE they carry no-need language, ~6× the corpus DQ rate by construction): **DQ 5 → 5, objection 10 → 10, `fear` did not drain** — the named red flag did not appear. ② **Exactly ONE flip**, and re-running that call **3× per arm** shows the **OLD** rule produces the same `disqualify_signal` at the **same 1-in-3 rate** — run variance on a borderline moment, **not attributable to this change**. ⚠ **A FIRST, NON-ADVERSARIAL SAMPLE OF 8 CALLS FOUND ZERO DQ IN EITHER ARM and is reported as a non-measurement, not a clean pass** — at 1.2% corpus DQ, 52 moments expects 0.6. ⚠ **EXPECT A SMALLER EFFECT THAN THE DESIGN SUGGESTS: the canonical $3M example is ALREADY stored as `disqualify_signal` under the OLD rules**, so v27 was catching a good share of these. The change buys consistency, not coverage. ⚠ **New calls only; the handle rate will drift UP as the corpus turns over** (drift note at `lib/tile-metrics.js objectionHandleRate`, cross-referencing v17). |

against a recorded 3,211 baseline. **No new model call — it rides the grader.** ⚠ I measured the wrong case first (six REPLACING derived, 3,073/68%) and that understated it; and the +215 is inside the ~300-token run-to-run variance, so treat it as approximate. ⚠ **v32 → v33, ADDITIVE, NO DELTA GATE** (the coverage block is barred from influencing any score — the v10 precedent). **CAPTURE ONLY; store-then-read before anything consumes it.** ✅ **RULED AND SHIPPED: fixed six PLUS derived.** `lib/discovery-areas.js`, present on every call for every rep, the six leading so a colliding derived key loses and the STABLE key wins. **Verified on a real 2,376-turn call from a rep with NO derived areas — where coverage is empty today: 6 of 6 covered, each with a verbatim line.** ⚠ **ONE UNAVOIDABLE CONSUMPTION: `what_mattered` validates against the same list so its candidate set grows — and scoping it to derived-only would have been WORSE, not safer, since picks from the six would be REJECTED.** ⚠ `why_now`'s evidence is the weakest of the six (it opens with what reads as the closer's question) — within contract, but the one to watch. **v32 → v33, additive, no delta gate. Capture only.** (superseded question: PLUS or INSTEAD — I recommended PLUS) — the derived areas are what `what_mattered` ranks — **but it is the difference between 76% and 68% of the token budget.** |

### N · THE DISCOVERY CHECKLIST — AND WHAT THE DISCOVERY SCORE ACTUALLY MEASURES (Justin, 2026-08-29 — **REPORT DONE, NOT BUILT**)

**Justin's six, and his definition: *"the point of discovery is to discover the things you need to know to DQ the prospect or close the deal."* Both outcomes are correct.**
**PAIN** *(⚠ with a caveat — some offers are a LOGICAL sale with no pain, and missing pain is not a failure there)* · **GOALS** · **CURRENT SITUATION** · **DECISION MAKERS** · **WHY NOW** · **FINANCIAL RESOURCES**

| item | state | what was measured |
|---|---|---|
| **What Discovery is graded against today** | ✅ **REPORTED** | **The whole criterion is ONE LINE:** *"emotional pain depth (not just surface problem), specific dollar/timeline commitment, decision-maker confirmed present, 'why now' urgency established"*. Against the six: **PAIN ✅ but MANDATORY with no logical-sale caveat · GOALS ❌ ABSENT · CURRENT SITUATION ❌ ABSENT · DECISION MAKERS ✅ · WHY NOW ✅ · FINANCIAL RESOURCES 🟡 partial** (a *commitment they made*, not whether they *can pay*). ⚠⚠ **Two of six are not looked for at all**, and a logical-sale offer is marked down today for missing pain that legitimately is not there. |
| **Replace the score, or inform it?** | ⚠ **RECOMMENDED: INFORM — and informing is a PREREQUISITE for ever deciding to replace** | **Different instruments.** The score is a calibrated 0-100 judgement with an anchored rubric and an explicit do-not-penalise-a-different-route rule; six booleans cannot produce that, and swapping them in breaks every trend, average and rep comparison built on `discovery_score` — history stops being comparable. ⚠ **But the checklist is the better instrument on the merits and that argues for building TOWARD replacement**: the recorded per-section noise floor is **±14 points**, so `discovery_score` is a weak signal, while six transcript-checkable booleans are stable. **Store them, read them, then decide** — the measure-then-read discipline that made `coverage`, `what_mattered` and `qualification_covered` work. |
| **One change that needs none of that** | ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-30 as v32 — OVER A TRIPPED GATE, ON JUSTIN'S RULING.** | **Add GOALS and CURRENT SITUATION to the criterion, and put the logical-sale caveat on PAIN.** The grader is not looking for two of the six today. ⚠⚠ **IT MUST NOT RIDE WITH v30, AND THE REASON IS CONFOUNDING RATHER THAN CAUTION.** v30 is ADDITIVE — a new column, a third model call, no existing prompt touched, **no delta gate needed** (the same reasoning that let v10 ship: *"an extractor-only field; grader scoring/outcome prompts are UNCHANGED"*). **This is the opposite: an edit to the grading criteria that moves existing scores by design, so it REQUIRES the standard delta gate** — overall score only, median of 3 dry-runs per sample, 3 samples across closed/follow_up/lost, ~5-point threshold, hard stop if an outcome flips. **Bundled, any score movement afterwards is unattributable.** ⚠ **AND EXPECT THE PAIN CAVEAT TO RAISE LOGICAL-SALE CALLS — that is the grade becoming CORRECT, not the grader going soft**, the same shape as the v3 calibration note. ⚠⚠ **GATE RE-READ 2026-08-30 — IT MEASURED NOTHING, AND TWO EARLIER CLAIMS OF MINE ARE WITHDRAWN. (1) THE SCORE MOVEMENT IS INSIDE THE DOCUMENTED NOISE**: overall-score drift between rounds on byte-identical input is ~6 points, and the deltas are +3/0/+3. **(2) THE PAIN CAVEAT WAS NEVER EXERCISED — none of the three samples is a LOGICAL-SALE call** (all three had pain surfaced or assessed), so the change's main predicted benefit is untested. ⚠⚠ **A GATE CAN RUN CORRECTLY AND ANSWER A DIFFERENT QUESTION: sample selection is part of its validity.** To actually decide this it needs at least one genuine logical-sale call and a read on `discovery_score` rather than overall. **RECOMMENDATION: do not ship on this evidence — not because the change looks wrong, but because the evidence does not speak to it.** Original gate result follows. **SCORES PASSED, AN OUTCOME MOVED.** `closed 78→81 (+3) · follow_up 65→65 (0) · lost 58→61 (+3)` — **all inside the ~5-point threshold and all upward, as predicted** — but the lost sample's OUTCOME flipped. **A six-run re-test per arm settled that it is ATTRIBUTABLE, not noise: the OLD criterion returned `follow_up` 6 of 6 and never once `lost`, while the NEW one returned `lost` 2 of 6.** ⚠⚠ **THE BIGGER FINDING: A DISCOVERY CRITERION HAS NO BUSINESS MOVING THE OUTCOME.** Discovery quality belongs in `discovery_score`; whether a deal closed, was lost or is open is a fact about what happened. **That it moves at all means the grader's outcome judgement is ENTANGLED with its section reading — a pre-existing property this change EXPOSED, not created.** ⚠ **A validation NOT claimed: the stored outcome on that call is `lost`, so the new criterion agrees with it more often — but `outcome_source` is `inferred`, so that is agreement with an EARLIER MODEL RUN, not with truth.** ⚠ **Corroborating the change on its merits: that call's production `why_outcome` already read *"no income goal, no emotional driver, no urgency"* — the grader was citing missing GOALS as decisive while the criterion never asked for goals.** ⚠ **WHICH OF THE SIX THIS COVERS: it ADDS the two that were absent — GOALS and CURRENT SITUATION — and caveats PAIN. DECISION MAKERS, WHY NOW and FINANCIAL RESOURCES were already there in some form** (the last only partially, as a dollar/timeline COMMITMENT rather than capacity to pay). ⚠⚠ **SHIPPED AS v32 2026-08-30, and the "attributable" claim is WITHDRAWN 2026-08-30.** Re-run, the new arm returned `follow_up` **4 of 4** with no `lost`; cumulative is **OLD follow_up 8/8, NEW follow_up 8/10, lost 2/10 — which supports nothing**. Six further calls, one run per arm: **1 disagreement of 6, running `lost` → `follow_up`, the OPPOSITE direction.** **Flips wobble both ways on borderline calls; the criterion is not pushing outcomes anywhere.** ⚠⚠ **THE REAL EFFECT IS ON THE FIELD THE CHANGE TARGETS: `discovery_score` +0/+4/+11/+12/+13/+10, mean +8.3, every one non-negative** — large, consistent, and far outside the noise that made the overall deltas meaningless. **The gate measured the wrong field and saw a phantom instead of the effect.** ⚠ **AND A FLIP COSTS A REP ALMOST NOTHING MEASURED**: the rendered close rate, `close_score` and the handle rate are all identical for `follow_up` and `lost`; only the manager digest reads differently (and a legacy per-call rate that is rendered nowhere). Original framing follows. **Justin overrode the gate KNOWINGLY.** **His reasoning: he is unsure this offer has ANY logical sales, so waiting for a gate sample that exercises the PAIN caveat is waiting for a case that may never occur — a test that cannot be run is not a reason to hold indefinitely.** ⚠ **WATCH, baseline captured immediately pre-ship: `lost:follow_up` = 0.137** (lost 9.5%, follow_up 69.1%) on the 1,000 most recently analysed real calls. **A rate materially above that is the predicted consequence arriving — STOP AND REPORT, do not tune it back.** ⚠ **Unlike v30/v31 this one MOVES SCORES BY DESIGN, so its outdated count is not merely cosmetic. New calls only; nothing re-grades.** |
| **⚠⚠ The checklist is the UPSTREAM CAUSE of the five objection types, not a separate thing** | ✅ **RECORDED** | **Spouse = DECISION MAKERS uncovered · timing = WHY NOW uncovered · the money gap = FINANCIAL RESOURCES uncovered · the fear reframe needs PAIN and GOALS.** An objection is a discovery item surfacing late. **Justin's definition of pain makes it a RELATION rather than a feeling: *"pain happens when your current situation doesn't align with your goals"*** — two of the six items, compared. |

| **(b) MOVING A REP DOES NOT MOVE THEIR HISTORY.** Call data stays with the company where it was EARNED. | ⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-25 entry that accepted COPYING history to the new team and accepted the resulting double-count.** Corrected here **and in `CLAUDE.md`** — both readings are not left on file. The consequence is unchanged: needs a team stamp at call creation plus a sweep of every team-scoped query. |
| **(c) THE DIGEST COMPLIANCE QUESTION: SUPPRESS.** Scout does not surface legal or liability matters **anywhere** — not as coaching, not elsewhere. | The open question ("suppress entirely, or surface away from coaching?") is **CLOSED, answer (a)**. **Do not build a second surface for it.** The row is now a buildable BUG. |
| **(d) THE DAILY DIGEST STAYS A DAY BEHIND.** Deliberate. | Closes the row. Generation rides the sync cron's post-sync pass, so each ET day is built ~00:45 the following morning and the newest tab is always yesterday. The two alternatives (generate on read; a much shorter cron) were costed and are not wanted. |
| **(e) A DELETED USER'S DATA IS REMOVED FROM THE SITE COMPLETELY.** | ⚠⚠ **ESTABLISHED BEFORE SCOPING, AND IT IS NEITHER OF THE TWO FAILURE MODES.** Not a cached surface, and **not a delete path that missed a table** — measured 2026-08-28, **0 orphan auth rows**, so `lib/user-purge.js` leaves no debris and any NEW delete already removes everything. What remains is **(i)** three legacy tombstones from the superseded contract and **(ii)** nothing anywhere filters `active`. ⚠⚠ **The ruling REACHES the three tombstones, but purging them destroys 117 calls / 117 analyses / 87 highlights / 56 prospects currently counted in Josh's team history, and they were deleted under a contract that promised the calls would stay — so that consequence is a SEPARATE decision and sits in NEEDS JUSTIN.** ⚠ Once they are gone the reported bug goes with them, and what is left is purely about DEACTIVATED users, whose numbers ruling (a) says should stay. |

**⚠⚠ THE PROCESS FAILURE THIS BLOCK EXISTS TO FIX, AND IT MATTERS MORE THAN THE FIVE ROWS: FIVE OF THESE HAD BEEN RULED BEFORE AND THE FILE STILL CARRIED THEM AS OPEN.** Justin answered three of them **twice**. **A decided question sitting in an open list gets re-asked.** Standing rule from now on: **when a ruling lands, the OPEN row is closed in the SAME edit** — the ruling and the closure are one change, never two.

### 🎫 The support arc — DESIGN RULINGS, now SHIPPED

**⚠ Kept because the design reasoning outlives the build.** The health snapshot (`a298652`), the ticket control and admin list (`b52fafb`), a person seeing their own tickets (`049d628`) and categories/screenshots (`c8d2f96`) all shipped 2026-08-27.

**⚠⚠ THESE NOW SIT ABOVE ZOOM, THE PASSWORD PAGE, THE ROLE HIERARCHY AND THE UNCONNECTED-SOURCE BADGE.** The evidence is this week's own record: **two support tickets out of a handful of beta users — Josh's Zoom, then Godwin's — and EVERY ONE needed the architect or Claude to query the database by hand to answer.** Godwin's took a server-log read to establish that **nothing was broken at all**. That does not scale past a handful of users, and it is not a reporting nicety — it is the only way a ticket gets answered.

**⚠ THE DEPENDENCY ORDER IS UNCHANGED AND IS THE POINT: the HEALTH SNAPSHOT IS BUILT FIRST. A ticket without it is just a message box.**

**⚠⚠ THE SNAPSHOT HAS NO UI (Justin's correction, standing).** It is **generated on demand and ATTACHED TO A TICKET** — the ticket list is the only surface a human ever sees. Do not design a health dashboard.

### ⚠⚠ THE SUPPORT CONTROL IS ON EVERY PAGE (Justin, 2026-08-26)
His words: *"it should be a tech support, submit a ticket or whatever you want to call it on EVERY PAGE they see."* Four things follow, and they decide the design:
1. **PERSISTENT CHROME — ONE CONTROL RENDERED ONCE AND SHOWN EVERYWHERE, NEVER COPIED ONTO EACH PAGE.** ⚠ This codebase has paid for the copy-per-surface mistake repeatedly and recently: **the objection label lists had to be unified TWICE** (a sweep missed three more), and **the company picker rendered on two surfaces and would have shown TWO on one page.** A control placed on ~15 pages by copying is that failure at maximum scale.
2. **THE TICKET CAPTURES WHICH PAGE THEY WERE ON.** ⚠ **Free context the app already has** — it turns *"X isn't working"* into *"X isn't working ON THE CALLS PAGE"*, and it costs nothing to record.
3. **IT MUST NOT OBSTRUCT ANYTHING.** A control on every page is on every page **forever** — it cannot cover content, steal a click target, or sit over a scroll region.
4. **⚠⚠ EVERY ROLE, NOT ADMINS. Closers are the ones who hit problems.** ⚠ **This is the opposite of the TICKET LIST, which stays ADMIN-ONLY because it exposes other companies' data** — same feature, two different permission answers, and conflating them leaks data. **The health snapshot still attaches automatically.**

**⚠ DEPENDENCY ORDER IS UNCHANGED: #6 before #7** — a ticket without the snapshot is just a message box. **Neither is urgent at 2 accounts; both become urgent at the first real customer.** Justin has given a priority only to #1; the rest sit at the bottom per his standing rule.

| # | item | priority | what it needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | **Account health SNAPSHOT — a function, NOT a page** | filed, prerequisite for #7 | ⚠⚠ **CORRECTED BY JUSTIN 2026-08-26: HE DOES NOT NEED A HEALTH REPORT SCREEN.** No admin page, no dashboard, nobody maintains it. It is a **function that generates a snapshot on demand when a ticket is raised**, and the snapshot is **attached to the ticket**. ⚠ That makes it **much smaller work than a page**, and **removes the admin-only permission surface a health page would have needed**. **Contents:** calls synced / graded / pending / errored + the **last error TEXT** · connection status and last successful sync per provider, with `last_sync_at` NULL or stale flagged loudly · whether the digest generated and whether it produced **zeros against real calls** · recent failures in enough detail to tell a **PROVIDER problem from a SCOUT problem**. ⚠⚠ **THE QUESTION IT EXISTS TO ANSWER: "is this ONE BROKEN ACCOUNT or a BROKEN PRODUCT?"** — John reports X at Company A and Jim reports Y at Company B on the same day; shared cause or two faults? Today that needs an engineer querying Supabase by hand. |
| 7 | **🎫 Tech Support ticket — THE ONLY VISIBLE SURFACE** | filed, **depends on #6** | A **Tech Support** control in Scout: describe the issue, attach screenshots, optionally paste a Loom link. ⚠⚠ **The TICKET LIST is the visible feature and the only thing Justin ever sees** — there is no health screen. ⚠ **What makes it worth building: the health snapshot is auto-attached to every ticket.** "X isn't working" is nearly useless; the same ticket carrying sync status, graded/pending/errored counts and the last error is often **self-answering**, because most reports will be a stalled sync, ungraded calls or a dropped connection. ⚠ The submitter is logged in — identity, company, role and account state are known. **NEVER ask the user for information Scout already has.** ⚠ **The ticket list stays ADMIN-ONLY, enforced server-side** — it exposes another company's data. ⚠ **ARCHITECT-READABLE (Justin's request):** he wants to say "check support" and have the architect read them, so open tickets must be **pullable to a file** (same pattern as `~/Desktop/scout-findings.md`) with **screenshots downloadable locally**; a Loom link is just a URL. ⚠ **Notification: Scout has NO notification system** — already filed as blocking EOD manager approval. This depends on that or on something simpler (email). |

Visible one call at a time (red badge + red banner with the reason), **invisible in aggregate** — there is no "3 calls failed today". **RECOMMENDED: count failed calls beside ungraded ones and let the existing control retry them** — no new screen, no alerting. ⚠ Caveat: some can never succeed (a Zoom call with no transcript), so show those separately or the count never reaches zero. |

Discriminator: **willing-but-unable vs able-but-hesitant**, and unable splits by why. **Spouse/Partner = anyone they must consult**, defined by the need to consult, not the relationship. ⚠ **My flag about `logistical` being both named and excluded is WITHDRAWN — that is correct and intentional**: a genuine inability is not a coaching failure, so it is counted and shown but kept out of the handle rate. ⚠ **CROSS-REF: this is the likely cause of the Abu failure above** — the DQ boundary being unclear to the grader, not a digest-only bug. |

Price folds into the three-way boundary: hesitant about money → **fear**; cannot move money now → **logistical**; genuinely cannot afford it → **financial DQ**, not an objection. **✅ SHIPPED:** the coercion maps it explicitly (`Trust / Proof / Skepticism → Fear`, `Price / too expensive → Fear`), pinned in tests. |

Costs almost nothing: 99% of real calls have matched speakers. ⚠ **The downgrade is ONE-WAY** — an unprovable quote turns a decided verdict into `undetermined`; nothing can ever turn anything INTO `failed`. ⚠ **ONE notion of a DQ**, fed into v27's existing outcome rule; no server-side forcing of `outcome`. ⚠ **Criteria are free text per company** — nothing hardcodes 640, and only 1 of 8 profiles has any, so the prompt is byte-identical for everyone else. **Truncation gate run on the two longest calls (2123/2097 turns) with production inputs: 2684 and 2419 of 4500, no truncation.** NO UI yet, by the standing store-then-read pattern |

Labels are now **coerced to a closed vocabulary**, longest 14 chars, so the cut **cannot fire**. ⚠ **`other` is the existing NULL** — the CHECK still allows only the four, so this is a **pure rename: no migration, no re-grade**. ⚠⚠ **The `NEEDS_WORK_LANE_VERSION` bump is load-bearing** — the labels live inside the cached payload, so without it the rename would have looked shipped and rendered the old names indefinitely. Coercion honours Justin's named mappings (`Trust / Proof / Skepticism → Fear`), 13 pinned against the REAL extracted function. ⚠ **The two surfaces still have different DENOMINATORS on purpose** — Focus counts true objections only, the drilldown counts every moment |
| **Justin's objection boundary, in the grader** | Cannot afford = a disqualification, not a coachable objection | shipped `eadd4af`, `ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION` v26 → **v27**. The prompt previously said the OPPOSITE of the ruling, so an affordability failure had **no route to a DQ**. Now one question precedes categorisation — *can the prospect buy if they decide to?* ⚠ **The DQ carve-out reuses `disqualify_signal`, an EXISTING type** — no enum, no migration. Shared guidance string means the extractor and the surface bucketer **cannot drift**. ⚠ **NO output-token gate needed**: input text plus a redirect between two existing types, output shape byte-identical. ⚠⚠ **The bump marks every prior analysis outdated and the control will offer a large re-grade — that count is COSMETIC. Do not click it.** Already-graded calls keep their old classifications by design |
| **Failed calls surfaced and retryable** | Two counts beside the ungraded one; the existing button now reaches failures | shipped `1af6cd1`. Before, a failed call was in **no count** and **no button could retry one**. ⚠⚠ **Split into retryable (red, an action) and permanent (neutral + a title saying why)** — folded together the number never reaches zero, and a number that never reaches zero stops being read. `lib/failure-class.js`: permanent = missing or rejected (no transcript / 404 / credentials); retryable = everything else **including exhausted automatic attempts**, since a human retry is a different act and gets a fresh budget. ⚠ **Fails OPEN, unlike model-retry and fathom-retry** — the failure direction follows the consequence (one wasted click vs hiding a recoverable call forever). ⚠ `zoom_no_transcript` still decided by `zoom-retry`'s age bound, not a second rule. **Verified on production:** both counts render; dry run reports `failed: 1`, so the retryable one is in and the permanent one is out. ⚠ **The full retry was NOT run** — the control is window-scoped, so retrying that one call meant 80 calls ≈ $19 and re-grading 79 that are fine |
| **Model-call retry** | A rate limit no longer permanently ungrades a call | shipped `263c3de`, migration 048. 429/5xx/529/connection/timeout → requeue (bounded 3); 400/401/403/404/422 → still permanent; **unusable output gets its own branch** (a 200, bounded 2, tighter because it is length-correlated and costs a full transcript per try). Clean run resets both counters. ⚠ **The SDK already retries 3× with backoff** — this sits on top, for a sustained burst not a blip. ⚠ **Separate counter from `transcript_attempts`** or a transcript flake spends the model's budget and the two become indistinguishable. ⚠ `requeueTranscript` reused (one requeue, three callers); `fathom-retry` NOT reused — it parses a status out of a string, the SDK gives one structurally. Tests drive the REAL branches; disabling them fails 3 of 7 |
| **Digest day navigation** | Prev Day / Next Day replaces the date chip row | shipped `9a96d62`, duplicate-date fix `190a421`. ⚠⚠ **The walk is BOUNDED to the 7 days the chips offered** — 35 digest days exist back to 07-21 and days 8+ predate the manager fix, reading "quiet day · 0 calls" for days Josh worked. **The 7-day cap was accidentally shielding the reader from 28 stale days.** Both directions; disabled not hidden at each end; Next cannot reach a day with no digest by construction. **Verified by clicking on production:** back 24→23→22→21, forward 21→22, stats matching the DB at each step (8, 1, 6, 13). ⚠ A duplicate date under the control was caught by the SCREENSHOT, not the tests |
| **Fathom transcript retry** | A 429 or 5xx used to mark a call `error` **permanently**, with nothing retrying it | shipped `582acbb` + migration 047. **Latent defect, not a consequence of the twelve-loop incident** — one bad minute on Fathom's side would silently hole any customer's data. ⚠ **Zoom had this fixed and Fathom did not**: `lib/zoom-retry.js` exists for the same class. ⚠ **Deliberately diverges** — Zoom bounds by CALL AGE (its two cases are textually identical); Fathom's failures carry an HTTP status, so age would permanently fail old calls on a transient blip. Bound is **attempt count**, cap 5, reset on a clean fetch. Retry-After parsed and clamped to 300s. Unclassifiable = permanent. **Proved by forcing a 429 through the REAL extracted branch**; disabling the retry fails all 7 |

The old "unsafe to delete a company that received a rep" warning is **withdrawn**. ⚠ **Not yet honourable in code**: `fathom_calls` carries only `user_id` and team totals resolve `managed_by` live, so "which company earned this call" is unanswerable today. A failed status fetch counts as a quiet tick, not a failure. **⚠ Repaints only the control host** — a full re-render every 15s replays every `.fade-in` |

## FILED 2026-09-01 — A STALE-BUNDLE NUDGE (not built, needs a ruling)
**Scout has no way to tell a browser that a newer build exists.** The dashboard is a single-page app: the HTML is `no-cache`, but **a tab that never navigated never re-fetched**, and `setView` only changes the hash. ⚠⚠ **So "the fix did not land" and "your tab is old" are INDISTINGUISHABLE to a user and to us** — and this is the second report that may be exactly that.

**The fix is a version marker plus a *"Scout has updated — reload"* prompt.** ⚠ It is a product decision (how insistent, whether it can force a reload, what it does mid-edit on an unsaved board) rather than a tidy-up. **Not built.**

⚠ **The interim workaround is a one-glance discriminator, and it should be produced every time a shipped fix is reported as absent:** `git show <old-sha>:<file>` and state what the OLD version looks like. It costs one command and removes a whole class of round trips.

## NEEDS JUSTIN 2026-09-01 — "ANY GRAPH SHOULD BE METRIC AGAINST TIME" IS IN TENSION WITH LAST BLOCK
**⚠⚠ NOTHING WAS DELETED ON THE INFERENCE, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** One block ago Justin asked for *"bar graphs (horizontal and vertical), SCATTER PLOT, line graphs"*; this block, *"any graph should be metric against time"*. **A bar chart by rep is not metric-against-time, and a scatter is metric against METRIC** — so the two cannot both be read literally.

| reading | means | consequence |
|---|---|---|
| **A** — a graph's x-axis is always time | `bar_rep`/`bar_cat` are not graphs and go; scatter never gets built | **seven of ten metrics can have NO graph at all** |
| **B** — "graph" means a line over time; bars and lists are different things | nothing retired; the ask is MORE things plotted over time | the work is giving the other seven a history |

**Reading B is the likelier one** (he asked for bar charts one block ago, and a scatter is explicitly metric-vs-metric) — **but it is a one-word ruling and the wrong guess deletes two card types that shipped yesterday.**

**⚠⚠ THE MEASURED PART, AND IT IS THE ANSWER TO "VERY CUSTOMIZABLE": giving the other seven a history is far cheaper than it looks.** `rep-series` already fetches calls (with `call_date` and `prospect_id`), analyses (with `outcome`) and objection highlights, bucketed per rep per week:
```
Calls graded              NOTHING — count the calls already in the bucket
People talked to          NOTHING — distinct prospect_id; the closing series already groups them
How calls ended           NOTHING — count by `outcome`, already fetched
Average call score        ONE COLUMN   overall_score        (1,555 of 1,589)
Average call length       ONE COLUMN   duration_seconds     (2,052 of 2,058)
Scores by part of call    FIVE COLUMNS
What Scout flagged        ⚠ 6.3x THE ROWS — 9,022 highlights vs the 1,424 objections fetched today
```
**Three are free, two are one column, one is five, one is a real cost.** ⚠ **A bar chart of a snapshot is not what he is asking for; a history is.**

⚠ **ONE CONSTRAINT THAT IS NOT A COST:** *Scores by part of the call* is FIVE series per rep — five lines × nine reps is unreadable, so it wants a different shape (one line per section, team-wide). **A design question, not a data one.**

**Justin: *"I don't know what those metrics do."* ⚠⚠ FIFTH INSTANCE OF A VALUE WHERE A LABEL BELONGS** — after `follow_up` on the EOD chip, "1000 chunks" on the KB, a manager's email in the admin dropdown, and raw seconds on the objections page. **`outcome_mix`, `moment_mix` and `section_scores` are INTERNAL FIELD NAMES PROMOTED TO A CUSTOMER-FACING PICKER.**

Recommend description under the name, views on the right.

## 2026-09-01 — TWO NEW CHART TYPES, DERIVED NOT LISTED (`4433ba2`)
Deployed, verified by commit hash and served-page parity. Suite **2051**.

**⚠ THE BRIEF'S PREMISE WAS WRONG AND IT WAS CHECKED: `by_rep` does NOT already draw bars** — it is a plain name/value list; the bars-per-rep component is `team-detail-track` on another surface. So horizontal bar is a genuinely new drawing, still cheap.

**(a)+(b) `bar_rep` (horizontal, per rep) and `bar_cat` (vertical, per category).** A new chart type is a **new capability requirement**, not a new list entry — both reuse requirements that already existed, so `viewsFor` decides who gets them and **a metric with no categories is structurally unable to be offered a category bar chart** (verified on the render).

**⚠ ORIENTATION IS FIXED PER DATA SHAPE AND MEASURED:** rep names 8-13 chars, up to nine (unreadable rotated); category names 4-10, four or five (what columns are for). **The drawing takes orientation as a parameter if it should become a free choice — a small follow-up, and it is Justin's call.**

**⚠⚠ NO CANVAS.** `trend` needs one for axes and several series over time; four to nine bars do not, and a canvas drags in the mount/destroy lifecycle that made the team graphs rebuild fifteen times on one visit.

**⚠⚠ THE SORT NOW FOLLOWS THE METRIC'S DIRECTION** — descending is wrong for a ceiling metric, and **a bar chart makes it far worse than a list because the longest bar reads as best.** No inverted metric reaches it today; closed before it can appear, and the card says "lower is better". **One ranking, two views**, so the list and the bar cannot drift. **A category with no objections draws no bar and says "none"** — a bar at zero is a different claim.

**⚠ LINE IS ALREADY BUILT — RENAMED, NOT REBUILT.** `trend` is now "Line graph over time". Two things answering one question is the most repeated defect here.

**(c) SCATTER — REPORTED, NOT BUILT.** It needs TWO metrics on one card: **38 references to a card's single `metric`**, `viewsFor(metric)` takes ONE object where a scatter needs the intersection of two, and the stored layout, both sanitisers, the picker flow and the unit table all assume one. **5 metrics qualify as an axis → 10 unordered pairs**; the shape that earns it is closing rate against calls taken, one point per rep. **Structural, not a drawing routine.**

**⚠ OPEN FOR JUSTIN, ONE LINE: the removal took SIX, not three** — his three plus outcome mix, section scores and call moment mix, which Scout CAN measure and has no card for. **Not restored unasked.**

**Guards:** three CONVERTED (the ranking moved to a shared function; both consumers must surface its refusal), plus one-ranking / no-canvas / scale-to-data. **5 of 5 restored defects caught.** ⚠ One was anchored on a LOOP VARIABLE NAME and broke on a rename it should not have cared about. ⚠⚠ And the scale guard tested the HELPER, not its call site — **fifth instance**, caught only because five restored defects produced four failures.

## 2026-09-01 — DELETE, SAVE AS NEW, RENAME — **CUSTOMIZE VIEW IS FINISHED** (`a32b3a5`, `2940bc5`)
Verified by commit hash and served-page parity. Suite **2071**. Six restored defects → 6 failures.

`new test board  PINNED   [Unpin] [Rename] [+ New Board] [✎ Edit]   [Delete]`

**DELETE.** The route existed and had no control — the pin's shape again. Confirmation **NAMES the board**, modal and awaited, no undo stated plainly with the card count. ⚠⚠ **The deleted id is cleared from state**: the load route falls through to the CODE DEFAULT when `?board=` matches nothing, so a stale id would show the default *even though other boards remain* — and read as "delete wiped my other boards". **Both edge cases proven LIVE on a FORK of Justin's board, never his own**: pinning the copy swapped the pin with no index collision; deleting it while pinned left no orphan pin, fell back to his board, and the nav named it; his pin was restored and checked against the database. ⚠ **The last-board case was NOT tested by deleting his only board** — the identical code path was exercised instead (an id matching nothing → `is_default: true`, six default cards, not an empty grid) **and the report says so.**

**SAVE AS NEW** forks by sending NO id; the copy is never pinned. ⚠ The cap message said *"Rename or delete one to make room"* — **renaming makes no room**, so it now names the action that works. ⚠⚠ **And the fork left the manager on the ORIGINAL** — a refetch of `boards[0]` — so adding a card and forking returned them to the board WITHOUT it, reading exactly as the changes being lost. **Found by exercising it, not reading it.**

**RENAME HAS ITS OWN ROUTE, REQUIRED BY AN EXISTING RULING.** `resolveLayout` drops an unknown card and deliberately does not write, so a rename through the save path would post the RESOLVED layout back and **permanently destroy the dropped entry** — silent loss that looks like success. `PATCH /dashboard/:id/name` touches `name` only; a guard asserts it never writes `layout`.

**FINISHED — BUILT:** ten metrics · seven views · honest offers · seven line graphs · two banded metrics with sides and band-coloured bars · grouped picker with a description on every metric · ten named boards · drag/resize · pin & unpin · rename · save-as-new · delete · 403 handled.
**FILED, NOT BUILT:** scatter · orientation · history for the three breakdown metrics · weekly bucketing above ~14 days · non-monotonic bars · rep-card widget · stale-bundle nudge · the `+`-label guard gap.
**NEEDS JUSTIN:** nothing on Customize View.

| `🗄 RETIRED 2026-09-02 (Justin: there is no round two — Josh's complaint was the black-and-white one, and the floating sidebar plus more mesh fixed it; "he loves it")` **THE DESIGN PASS — SECOND ROUND, NOT A FIRST** (was NEXT, Josh: *"generic and too black-and-white"*) | ⚠⚠ **READ THIS BEFORE STARTING: A DESIGN PASS ALREADY RAN AND CLOSED TODAY** (`683602c`, `4b4a033`) — the entries are in this file above. **Josh's complaint is FEEDBACK ON THAT PASS.** A session reading only the outstanding list would start one believing none had happened and redo shipped work. **ALREADY SHIPPED BY IT:** the opaque GROUND behind the content column (this is what makes text safe over the full-brightness raster — **do NOT solve exposure by dimming the artwork**, that is the recorded rule), cards removed on Performance/Coaching/Team, the green-bar pattern removed where it encoded nothing, one page black (`#0a0a0a`), the seven-size type scale, "My Account", admin monospace narrowed to transcription surfaces. **STANDING CONSTRAINTS A SECOND PASS MUST NOT VIOLATE:** the palette's three jobs (semantic / interactive / categorical, and the categorical ramp may borrow none of the reserved hues); **green deliberately means good AND brand AND interactive — that overlap is the design and is not to be re-raised**; the no-grey rule targets unreadable body text and **explicitly permits opacity as a depth cue** (the calendar exception); the type scale is CLOSED at seven sizes with two named exceptions (`--fs-gauge-value`, `--fs-company`) and **a third landing on 24px is the point to re-open the scale, not to add a fourth carve-out**; and every rule lives in `dashboard.html` inline plus `css/style.css`, **which are pinned identical by `test/type-scale.test.js` because the dashboard links no stylesheet**. ⚠ **A NEW VIEW INHERITS NOTHING** — the ground and cards-off lists are per-view and the pairing guard cannot see a view absent from both. |

Verified by commit hash and served-page parity. Suite **2066**. Five restored defects → 4 failures.

Now `DELETE /team/dashboard/:id/pin`, one handler for both directions, rendered beside the board selector that already marks the pinned one. **The badge states the STATUS, the button states the ACTION** so neither restates the other. ⚠ **Unpinning falls back to the most recently updated board and the nav names it — confirmed by a live round trip, restored to the original state and checked against the database.**

## 2026-09-01 — BOTH BANDS LIVE, AND THE DROPDOWN BUG WAS A LABEL (`3d03a0b`)
Verified by commit hash and served-page parity. Suite **2062**. Five restored defects → 6 failures.

**THE BAND SHIPPED.** `avg_call_time` ideal **35–45**, acceptable **20–60** (both edges RULED). `time_to_price` **20–45** — ⚠ **lower MEASURED** (discovery items by price minute: 15–20 → 2.60, 20–25 → 3.23; the step sits at 20, derived here and **not** copied from call time; **partly mechanical** and the code says so), **upper RULED, not measured** (the data could not show one — 13 calls above 55 minutes). **Proof on the live board: Nathan moved from FIRST to FOURTH with the row reading UNDER**, and the gauge went from *"at or below 60 min"* to **"2 of 8 reps in the 35–45 min sweet spot"**. `time_to_price` gains the ranked views — a band supplies the order a direction could not, so **all six originally-missing combinations are open**.

**⚠⚠ THE SIDE WENT IN THREE PLACES.** Rows state it; reps inside the band tie and are listed rather than ranked; **and the bar is coloured by the band, which is the one that matters most** — bar length encodes magnitude, and under a band magnitude is not goodness. **One definition in `lib/metric-band.js`, both consumers importing, guarded so neither can redeclare the edges.**

| `▱ OBSERVATION` **THE BANDED BAR CHART'S BARS ARE NOT MONOTONIC** (2026-09-01) | Rows are ordered by DISTANCE from the band; bars are drawn from the VALUE. Nothing on screen is wrong — colour and side both read correctly — but a bar chart whose bars do not descend is unusual. **Sorting bars by value loses the urgency order; drawing length as distance loses the value.** Neither is obviously right. **Reported, not fixed.** |

**§4 — THE DROPDOWN BUG: THE SAVE WORKED.** Not a refresh problem (the menu rebuilds on every open) and not a wiring one — the row was in the database, named, six cards, `pinned = false`. **The entry required `pinned` for its LABEL** while the server returns `boards[0]` pinned-first-then-most-recent, so it always OPENED that board and refused to name it. Now the label names its destination; **position still follows the pin and there is still exactly one entry**. ⚠ **Unpinned boards were never unreachable** — the Customize page has its own selector listing every board by name.

## 2026-09-01 — A TARGET BAND: REPORTED, NOT BUILT (no commit — the model change is Justin's to rule on)
Justin ruled that neither faster nor slower is better for call length: *"a good sales call lasts anywhere from 20 min to over an hour… typically 35–45 min is the sweet spot."* **The catalog has only `higher_is_better` and `lower_is_better`, and neither is true.**

| `▰ MAJOR` **A TARGET BAND — a third shape for the direction model** (**NEEDS JUSTIN**, reported 2026-09-01) | ⚠⚠ **THE CEILING IS ALREADY RANKING A REP FIRST FOR RUSHING: Nathan reads BEST on call time at 28.8 min with 13 of 27 calls under 20 minutes.** Excluding no-shows moves it 28.8→29.9, so the cheap fix is ruled out. ⚠ **The ruling contains TWO bands** — acceptable 20–60, ideal 35–45 — and one pair of numbers loses that; a strict 35–45 puts **6 of 8 reps outside**. Two edges map onto the three colour zones `band()` already returns. ⚠⚠ **Ranking by distance alone REPRODUCES the defect**: 28.8 and 67.1 are both ~16 min out and need opposite coaching, so each row must state its side; reps inside the band are listed, not ranked. ⚠ **Five consumers, one of them a shared carrier — `lib/team-averages.js` declares call-time's direction separately from the catalog and must move with it.** |
| `▰ MINOR` **IS A LATE "MINUTES TO PRICE" ALSO A PROBLEM?** (**NEEDS JUSTIN**, 2026-09-01) | Ranked views stay refused. **No band was inferred from the call-length ruling** — that ruling is about call length and says nothing about when a price should land. Measured (n=427): price time correlates with call length only **0.523**, and **predicts discovery better than length does (0.367 vs 0.268)** — so it is its own metric, not a proxy. Discovery items rise monotonically 2.4 → 5.2 with a later price, **with no visible upper end — and the sample cannot show one, since the buckets above 55 min hold 13 calls between them.** The question: *is there a point where pricing too late is a problem, or is later always better?* |

**§3 — an early price DOES mean skipped discovery, with the caveat as part of the finding.** Items established of six, by price time: **2.4 (<20 min) → 3.3 → 3.3 → 3.9 → 5.2 (55–75 min)**. ⚠ Partly mechanical — a call that prices at minute 10 has had ten minutes to do discovery — so it is a real relationship and not evidence of causation. ⚠ Close-rate-by-duration was **deliberately not offered** as evidence: it is a recorded refused inference (a call runs long *because* it closed).

**Nothing was built or pushed.**

## 2026-09-01 — THE CATALOG IS FINISHED: FOUR MORE LINE GRAPHS, AND STEP TWO SAYS WHICH VIEWS MOVE (`d49a631`)
Deployed, verified by commit hash **and** served-page byte parity. Suite **2052**. **Looked at, signed in, on a real board.**

**§1 — THE BAR CHARTS STAYED, AND THE DISTINCTION IS NOW ON SCREEN.** *"Every graph is metric against time"* was read as *"when I say GRAPH I mean a line over time"*, not as a mandate to retire `bar_rep` and `bar_cat`. **Nothing was deleted on an inference.** Step two is grouped into **Over time** and **Right now**, so a manager choosing between a line graph and a bar by rep does not have to work out that one moves and one does not. Confirmed by clicking, 4 mousedowns delivered.

**§2 — HISTORY WAS THE ANSWER, NOT MORE CHART TYPES. TREND-CAPABLE METRICS 3 → 7.** Seven of ten metrics were snapshots, so seven of ten could never have a line — that, and not the chart list, is why the catalog felt thin. **Cost measured before anything was built:** `calls_analyzed` and `prospects` cost **nothing** (rows already fetched and bucketed), `avg_score` and `avg_call_time` cost **one column each** on selects already being made. **No new stored columns, no migration.** The three breakdown metrics are filed: `outcome_mix` and `section_scores` are five series not one, and `moment_mix` is a **6.3x row cost** (9,022 highlights against 1,424 objections).
- ⚠ **`prospects` REUSES THE CLOSING DENOMINATOR** — people who had a real conversation, no-shows and DQs dropped — because the number card reads that, and a graph beside a card must not disagree about the same word.
- ⚠ **A GAP IN THE DATA IS A GAP IN THE LINE, NEVER A ZERO.** A rep with no calls that day breaks the line; 0 would read as "scored zero".
- **A LIVE DEFECT FELL OUT OF IT:** the team baseline was selected by MATCHING THE AXIS LABEL, so the objection-handling card drew the **CLOSING** team average. Unit and series key are now passed; the inference is **removed rather than kept as a fallback**.

**⚠⚠ AND THE GUARD THAT SHOULD HAVE EXISTED DID NOT — restoring `history:false` on four metrics produced ZERO failures.** Three links now asserted in both directions: catalog claims history → `DASH_CANVAS` maps a series key → `rep-series` emits it **per rep AND on the team line**. Break any one and the card draws an empty chart, which reads as *"this rep had no calls"* rather than as a missing feature.

**§3 — METRIC NAMES: PROPOSED, NOTHING WIRED. ⚠ AWAITING JUSTIN.** Every description read out of the code that computes it — numerator, denominator, unit. The seven live ones plus the three he removed, renamed so he rules on what they ARE rather than what they were called (*"Call moment mix"* → *"What Scout flagged"*). Full table in the findings report.

**§4 — SCATTER: FILED, GENUINELY STRUCTURAL.** Two metrics on one card against **38 places that assume one** — it is a second dimension through the spine, not a chart type. **§5 — ORIENTATION: FIXED, FILED.**

**§6 — THE CATALOG IS FINISHED, WITH ONE HOLE NAMED.**

| `▰ MINOR` **THE NUMBER CARD AND THE BY-REP LIST CANNOT RENDER MINUTES** (measured 2026-09-01 — **NOT BUILT**) | `avg_call_time` offers only Gauge · Line; `time_to_price` offers only Line. **Six missing combinations**, refused honestly rather than drawn wrong — but *"minutes per call, per rep"* is a thing a manager asks for. **Teaching `dashNumberHtml` and `dashByRepHtml` the `min` unit is the cheapest remaining win in the catalog.** Both already carry a `DASH_UNIT` concept. |
| `▱ OBSERVATION` **A 30-DAY WINDOW BUCKETS DAILY** — a 9-rep chart is 270 points. Legible (the axis thins its own labels) but dense; weekly buckets above ~14 days would read better. **Pre-existing, reported not acted on.** |

**STILL OUTSTANDING, NOT IN THIS BLOCK:** rep-card widget, save-as-new, rename-a-board, the metric audit, the stale-bundle nudge, the design pass, the site sweep, Scout AI.

## 2026-09-01 — THE "NOT AVAILABLE YET" LIST REMOVED; THE GRAPHS ARE THERE (`3418197`)
